Let me first set up the directories, then follow the workflow step by step.

The input file has:
- A `merge` function (forward declared with contract)
- A `multi_merge` function that has the loop with `/* INFILL */` placeholder

The loop body:
```c
while (u) {
    if (y) {
        t -> next = y;
        y -> prev = t;
        t = y;
        u -> prev = t;
        y = y -> next;
        if (y) {
            y -> prev = 0;
        }    
    }
    else {
        u = merge(u , z);
        t -> next = u;
        return x;   
    }
    if (z) {
        t -> next = z;
        z -> prev = t;
        t = z;
        u -> prev = t;
        z = z -> next;
        if (z) {
            z -> prev = 0;
        }
    }
    else {
        u = merge(u , y);
        t -> next = u;
        return x;
    }
    t -> next = u;
    u -> prev = t;
    t = u;
    u = u -> next;
}
```

Let me understand the loop:
- Pre-loop: `t = x; u = t->next;` — so `t` points to `x`, `u` points to `x->next`
- The loop runs while `u != 0`
- If `y` is non-null: splice `y` between `t` and `u`, advance `y`
- If `y` is null: merge `u` with `z`, return `x`
- If `z` is non-null: splice `z` between `t` and `u`, advance `z`
- If `z` is null: merge `u` with `y`, return `x`
- Then: connect `t->next = u`, `u->prev = t`, `t = u`, `u = u->next`

This is merging three sorted lists by interleaving elements from `y` and `z` between elements of `x`.

At the start of the loop:
- `x` is the head of the result list
- `t` is the current tail
- `u` is the next element to process from `x`
- `y` is the current element from the second list
- `z` is the current element from the third list

The invariant should describe the state of all three lists being processed.

Let me first create the workspace and tmp directories, then run the unroller.

The issue seems to be fundamental to how this code is structured. The function `multi_merge` calls `merge(y,z)` at the end, which requires `dlistrep_shape(y,0)` and `dlistrep_shape(z,0)` as preconditions. But during the loop body execution, `y` and `z` may have been modified (or traversed) — the precondition variables `y` and `z` have been changed. This is getting very complex. Let me take a step back and think about what the invariant should be.

The `multi_merge` function merges three sorted lists by interleaving:
- It starts with list `x` (assuming `x != 0`)
- `t` points to the current tail node
- `u` points to the next node from `x` to process
- It takes one node from `y` (if y is non-null), splices it between `t` and `u`
- Then takes one node from `z` (if z is non-null), splices it between `t` (=old y) and `u`
- Then advances `t` to `u`, `u` to `u->next`
- Repeats

- `x` is the head of the result list
- `t` is the current end of the result portion being built
- `u` is the next `x`-node to process (u = t->next at loop head? Not exactly — after the loop body, `t = u` and `u = u->next`, so at the next iteration head, `t` is the old `u` and `u` is `t->next`)

```c

    // loop body
    ...
    // end of loop body:
    t -> next = u;
    u -> prev = t;
    t = u;
```

At the start of the loop:
- `t = x`, `u = x->next`
- `t->next == u` (this is an invariant, since before the loop, t=x, u=x->next)

Inside the loop body, `t->next` is temporarily changed (spliced to y or z), but at the end of the iteration, `t->next = u` restores it, and then `t = u`, `u = u->next`.

So at the next iteration head:

- `u` is `t->next` (another node from x)
- The invariant `t->next == u` holds

After the loop (when u == 0), we have `t` pointing to the last node, and `u == 0`.

Now, the three lists:
- `x`: head of the merged result. Never changes.

- `z`: head of the remaining z list (advances as nodes are consumed)

1. The merged result: from `x` to `t` — but `t` is not the end; after `t`, the list continues with `u` (since `t->next == u`). Before the loop:
- `t = x`, `u = x->next`, `x->next == u`

- `t->next = u` (restores link)
- `u->prev = t` (restores back-link)

YES, at the loop head, `t->next == u`.

- Before the loop: `dlistrep_shape(y, 0)` and `dlistrep_shape(z, 0)` — complete lists
- After one iteration (with y and z non-null): one node from y is consumed (spliced between old_t and old_u), one node from z is consumed (spliced between the y node and old_u). So:

Now, the key structural insight:
- The merged result from `x` to `u` (exclusive) is a segment that includes all processed nodes
- From `u` onward, the original x list continues
- `y` and `z` are the remaining y and z lists

Let me think about how to represent this with dllseg and dlistrep predicates.

The invariant should be:

- `dlistrep_shape(u, t)`: the list from `u` onward, with prev `t` (since `u->prev == t`)
- `dlistrep_shape(y, 0)`: the remaining y list
- `dlistrep_shape(z, 0)`: the remaining z list

Actually, looking at the reference for dllseg_shape:
```
dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y)
```
Where yp is the LAST NODE in the segment, and y is the endpoint. The last node in the segment is `yp` (= t in our case). Strategies unfold from the FIRST argument (x), not from yp. So to unfold to t, strategies need to unfold from x all the way through.

But wait — `dlistrep_shape(u, t)` has `u` as arg[0]. Strategies can unfold from `u`. `dlistrep_shape(u, t)` unfolds to `data_at(u->next, ...) * data_at(u->prev, ..., t) * dlistrep_shape(u->next, u)`. ```

To get `data_at(t->next, ...)`, strategies need to unfold from x until reaching t. But t might be deep in the segment (after many spliced y and z nodes). And the loop body uses `t->next = y` — it writes to `t->next`. QCP needs the `data_at` for `t->next` to allow writing.

If t (= yp) is 0, then dllseg_shape(x, 0, 0, u). The empty case requires x == u AND 0 == 0 (which is trivially true). This is a valid segment — it just says the chain from x to u (exclusive) forms a list where the last node before u has prev=0. But if x is the first node (prev=0), then the segment could be the complete list (all nodes have correct prev/next links), and the last node's prev would be some non-zero value, not 0. Actually, this isn't right. Let me re-read: yp is the LAST NODE in the segment, and the definition says the segment's last node yp has yp->next == y. The parameter yp is used to remember which node is the last one — it's a fixed node. The prev of yp is determined by the node before it in the chain.

So in the non-empty case, we unfold from x, passing yp (unchanged) through each recursive call, until we reach the node where we're at y (the endpoint). The node before y is yp, and yp->next == y. The prev of yp is set by the unfolding (as the x from the previous recursion level) — it's NOT equal to yp_prev directly.

Wait, I'm confused. Let me re-read:

```
dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y) = 
  (x == y && xp == yp && emp) || 
  exists z, data_at(field_addr(x, next), struct list*, z) * 
            data_at(field_addr(x, prev), struct list*, xp) * 
            dllseg_shape(z, x, yp, y)
```

- x: current node to expand
- xp: x's prev value
- yp: the last node (fixed, unchanged through recursion)
- y: endpoint marker (fixed, unchanged through recursion)

In each expansion:
- x's next = z, x's prev = xp
- Then continue from z with x as its prev

When we reach x == y (i.e., the current node equals the endpoint), the empty case fires: x == y && xp == yp. This means x's prev == yp, which means the node before y (which is yp, the last node in the segment) has yp->next == y. And yp's prev is whatever the previous expansion set it to (which is the node before yp in the chain).

So when x == y, we have:
- x (== y) is the endpoint — NOT in the segment
- xp == yp — the prev of the endpoint is the last node in the segment
- This means the last node in the segment (yp) has yp->next == y (by construction)

OK, so `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u)` means:
- Segment from x to u (exclusive), x->prev = 0, last node in segment = t, t->next = u
- The segment includes x (prev=0) and all intermediate nodes up to and including t
- t->next == u
- u is NOT in the segment

This perfectly describes the merged result portion through t.

Now, about `t != 0`: If t == 0, this would mean the segment's last node is 0 (NULL). But then t->next == u would be 0->next == u, which doesn't make sense. Since t is a node that's actually in the list, t != 0 is always true in any valid state.

    At the start of the loop: u is old_u. u->prev = old_u->prev (which was set by the previous iteration, and originally by `u->prev = t` at the END of the previous iteration).
    
    Then in this iteration: `u->prev = t` is called twice (once with t=y_node, once with t=z_node). So old_u->prev gets set to z_node.
    
    Then `t = u; u = u->next`: t = old_u, u = old_u->next. Now t->prev = old_u->prev = z_node. And t->next = old_u->next = u (since this is the original x list, old_u->next is the next x node = u).

So after one iteration:
- t = old_u
- t->prev = z_node (the last spliced node)
- t->next = u (= old_u->next)
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t) = dllseg_shape(x, 0, z_node, old_u)
  - Segment from x to z_node (inclusive), endpoint = old_u
  - Nodes: x, y_node, z_node. z_node->next = old_u.
  - Correct!
- dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) = dlistrep_shape(u, old_u)
  - List from u to end, u->prev = old_u
  - Correct!

And the field expressions `t->prev` and `t->next` are different fields — no dup_data_at. 

But wait — does this invariant handle the case where t->next might not be a separate list but actually part of the merged structure? At the loop head, t->next should be u (because of `t->next = u` at the end of the previous iteration). And u->prev = t. So t->next and u are the same node, and u->prev and t are linked.

In the invariant `dlistrep_shape(t->next, t)`, the first argument is `t->next` which evaluates to `u`. Since `u == t->next` at the loop head, this is `dlistrep_shape(u, t)` — which matches our earlier formulation at the level of values. The difference is that `t->next` is a field expression that re-evaluates when `t` changes.

- x != u (since u = x->next != 0 for the loop body), so non-empty case
- data_at(x->next, ..., z) * data_at(x->prev, ..., 0) * dllseg_shape(z, x, x, u)
- z = x->next = u
- dllseg_shape(u, x, x, u): u == u (endpoint match), x == x (prev match) → empty → emp
- Total: data_at(x->next, ..., u) * data_at(x->prev, ..., 0)

So at entry, the dllseg unfolds to exactly the needed data_at for x->next (= t->next since t = x).

- `u` in arg[3] of dllseg — this is a variable, QCP creates stack binding
- `u->prev` in arg[2] of dllseg — field expression, creates data_at for u->prev
- `u->next` in arg[0] of dlistrep — field expression, creates data_at for u->next
- `u` in arg[1] of dlistrep — variable used as value, no extra data_at

So this gives:
- data_at for u->prev (from the field expression)
- data_at for u->next (from the field expression)
- data_at for x (from arg[0] of dllseg)
- data_at for y (from arg[0] of dlistrep)
- data_at for z (from arg[0] of dlistrep)
- Stack bindings for u, x, y, z

But where is `data_at(u->prev, ...)`? This IS in u->prev field expression. And `t` is NOT directly represented — t = u->prev at the loop head.

Now the key test: can the loop body access what it needs?

1. `t->next = y`: t = u->prev. data_at(t->next, ...) = data_at((u->prev)->next, ...). From the dllseg: the last node is u->prev, which has next = u (endpoint). So data_at is inside the dllseg — needs unfolding.

2. `y->prev = t`: y is arg[0] of dlistrep → y's prev is accessible through unfolding.

3. `u->prev = t`: u->prev is a field expression → data_at ALREADY exists! QCP can directly access u->prev!

Wait, but u->prev appears in two places:
- As arg[2] of dllseg → the segment's last node is named u->prev (= t)
- As a field expression `<field_expr>` → QCP creates data_at(u->prev, ...) for this

Hmm, the field expression `u->prev` in dllseg_shape(x, 0, u->prev, u) — does QCP create a separate data_at for u->prev, or does it treat it as an identifier for the segment's last node?

Looking at the "Get Invariant" output for my previous invariant `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t)`, QCP created:
- `data_at(&(t_329_value->prev), struct list*, t_value_prev_328)` — explicit data_at for t->prev

This is FROM the field expression t->prev. So YES, field expressions create explicit data_at in the invariant!

Similarly, `u->prev` in `dllseg_shape(x, 0, u->prev, u)` would create `data_at(u_value->prev, ...)` explicitly, and `u->next` in `dlistrep_shape(u->next, u)` would create `data_at(u_value->next, ...)` explicitly.

So in the invariant state:
- data_at(u->prev, ..., t_val): gives permission for u->prev
- data_at(u->next, ..., next_val): gives permission for u->next
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, u->prev, u): segment with last node = u->prev, but the data_at for u->prev is also explicit!
  - DUP! The data_at for u->prev appears BOTH from the field expression AND from the dllseg unfolding!

This is why SKILL.md strategy 2 says: "Check for redundant data_at: A field expression in SEP or PROP creates an implicit data_at for that field. If a SEP predicate already encodes the same field (e.g., dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u) implies t->next == u), the field expression creates a duplicate."

So if I use `u->prev` as a field expression in the dllseg, and the dllseg INTERNALLY produces data_at for u->prev through unfolding, there's a dup!

BUT: does the dllseg unfolding happen at invariant CHECK time or at STRATEGY application time? If the dup is only detected after strategies unfold the dllseg, and the invariant entry check doesn't unfold, then the dup might not be caught at entry. But during loop body execution, QCP would have BOTH explicit data_at (from field expression) AND unfolded data_at (from strategy application) — dup at that point!

Hmm, this is getting really complex. Let me just try the invariant with field expressions on both sides and see what happens:

```
/*@ Inv Assert
    dllseg_shape(x, 0, u->prev, u) *
    dlistrep_shape(u->next, u) *
    dlistrep_shape(y, 0) *
    dlistrep_shape(z, 0)
 */
```

At entry: u = x->next. u->prev = x. u->next = x->next->next.

Let me trace through the invariant at entry:
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, x, u): segment from x to u, last node = x (u->prev)
  - Field expression u->prev creates data_at for u->prev (which is &(x->next->prev) = &(u->prev))
  - Hmm, u->prev = &(u->prev) = the prev field of the node u points to
  - This is a HEAP field, not a stack field

Actually wait, u->prev is a dereference: *(u).prev. So it's the HEAP field at address u + prev_offset. The data_at would be data_at(&(u->prev), struct list*, value).

And dllseg_shape(x, 0, u->prev, u):
- arg[2] = u->prev: this is the VALUE of u->prev, i.e., the pointer value stored in u's prev field. This value is the LAST NODE in the segment.
- So u->prev gives us the node that's before u in the list = t.

The field expression `u->prev` creates data_at for the heap field. The predicate argument `u->prev` uses its VALUE as a node identifier. So the field expression data_at is for the field ITSELF, and the predicate uses the field's VALUE.

These are DIFFERENT things:
- data_at for `u->prev` = data_at for the heap cell at address (u + prev_offset)
- predicate argument `u->prev` = the VALUE stored in that cell (= the address of the node before u)

So there's NO dup! The data_at is for the field, and the predicate uses the field's value. They coexist peacefully.

OK let me just try this invariant.

  — QCP is looking for data_at(t->next, ...)

Key observation: the source state has `data_at(&(u_332_value->prev), ..., u_value_prev_331)` and `t_326_value == u_value_prev_331`. So t = u->prev (as a VALUE). But the data_at is for u->prev (the field), not for t->next. t is a SEPARATE pointer that happens to have the same value as u->prev.

- u_value_prev_331->next = u_332_value (= u)

Can strategies somehow derive `data_at(&(u_value_prev_331->next), ...)` from this state? The target says `exists ... data_at(&(t_326_value->next), ...)`. Since t_326_value == u_value_prev_331, this is asking for `data_at(&(u_value_prev_331->next), ...)`.

- z = x->next = u
- dllseg_shape(u, x, x, u): u == u (true), xp = x, yp = x → x == x → true → empty → emp
- Result: data_at(x->next, u) * data_at(x->prev, 0)

data_at(x->next, u) = data_at(t->next, u) since t = x at entry. So unfolding DOES give t->next!

So if I use plain variables (no field expressions), the invariant only has the folded predicates and stack bindings. The field data_at's are only produced when strategies unfold the predicates.

And the strategies DON'T unfold predicates for assignment pre-condition checking (as we've seen from the errors).

The ONLY way to get data_at for a field in the invariant state is through FIELD EXPRESSIONS in predicate arguments.

So the invariant MUST use field expressions for every field that the loop body explicitly accesses. The fields are:
- t->next (written: `t->next = y`, `t->next = z`, `t->next = u`)
- u->prev (written: `u->prev = t`)
- y->prev (written: `y->prev = t`, `y->prev = 0`)
- y->next (read: `y = y->next`)
- z->prev (written: `z->prev = t`, `z->prev = 0`)
- z->next (read: `z = z->next`)

For y and z: `dlistrep_shape(y, 0)` where y is arg[0]. Unfolding gives data_at(y->next, ...) and data_at(y->prev, ..., 0). But this is FOLDED in the invariant. QCP needs the fields as field expressions for explicit data_at in the invariant.

But wait — the loop body accesses y->prev, y->next, z->prev, z->next through program variables (not field expressions in the invariant). The invariant says `dlistrep_shape(y, 0)`. When the loop body does `y->prev = t`, QCP needs data_at(y->prev, ...). From the invariant: y is arg[0] of dlistrep_shape. QCP tries to unfold... but can't!

Actually wait — maybe dlistrep_shape IS unfolded by strategies! Let me check the log for rule (dll_nodata, 3) which is the non-empty unfold for dlistrep. I noticed it wasn't applied for the `t->next = y` check. But maybe it IS applied for y and z?

Hmm, actually, looking at the rules applied:
1. (builtin/common.strategies, 12) at 1162
2. (dll_nodata, 4) at 1527 — dlistrep EMPTY unfold (arg[0] == 0)
3. (dll_nodata, 4) at 1763 — dlistrep EMPTY unfold again
4. (builtin/common.strategies, 14) at 1967

(dll_nodata, 4) unfolds dlistrep when arg[0] == 0. This was used when checking the branch where y == 0 (the `else` branch of `if(y)`).

(dll_nodata, 3) is the NON-EMPTY unfold of dlistrep (arg[0] != 0). It was NEVER applied! Why?

Maybe rule 3 is only triggered by specific targets. The target for `t->next = y` is `undef_data_at(t->next, ...)`. Rule 3 (dlistrep unfold) produces `data_at(t->next, z) * data_at(t->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(z, t)`. The data_at(t->next, z) would match the target. But rule 3 is not triggered.

Maybe the strategy system tries rules in a specific order, and rule 3 is lower priority. Or maybe rule 3 requires a specific target pattern that `undef_data_at` doesn't match.

I think the fundamental problem is that the built-in assignment execution mechanism doesn't properly interact with the strategy system. It looks for exact data_at matches and, failing that, tries some limited strategies. The full set of dll_nodata strategies (including rule 3) might not be in scope for this kind of check.

#include "/home/tzh66/qcp_skill/infra/dll_nodata_def.h"

struct list *merge(struct list *x , struct list *y)
/*@ With x_prev
    Require dlistrep_shape(x,x_prev) * dlistrep_shape(y,0)
    Ensure  dlistrep_shape(__return,x_prev)
 */;

struct list *multi_merge(struct list *x , struct list *y, struct list *z)
/*@ Require dlistrep_shape(x,0) * dlistrep_shape(y,0) * dlistrep_shape(z,0)
    Ensure  dlistrep_shape(__return,0)
 */
{
    struct list *t,*u;
    if (x == 0) {
      t = merge(y,z);
      return t;
    }
    else {
      t = x;
      u = t->next;
      /*@ Inv Assert exists p,
          dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) *
          dlistrep_shape(y, 0) *
          dlistrep_shape(z, 0) &&
          t == u->prev
       */
      while (u) {
        if (y) {
          t -> next = y;
          y -> prev = t;
          t = y;
          u -> prev = t;
          y = y -> next;
          if (y) {
            y -> prev = 0;
          }
        }
        else {
          u = merge(u , z);
          t -> next = u;
          return x;
        }
        if (z) {
          t -> next = z;
          z -> prev = t;
          t = z;
          u -> prev = t;
          z = z -> next;
          if (z) {
            z -> prev = 0;
          }
        }
        else {
          u = merge(u , y);
          t -> next = u;
          return x;
        }
        t -> next = u;
        u -> prev = t;
        t = u;
        u = u -> next;
      }
  }
  u = merge(y,z);
  t -> next = u;
  if (u) {
    u -> prev = t;
  }
  return x;
}
ENDOFFILE

Here is a summary of the work done on `multi_merge.c`:

**File:** `/home/tzh66/loop-invariant-inference/dataset_qcp_infill/System/GlibC/changed_codes/list/multi_merge/multi_merge.c`
**Result directory:** `/home/tzh66/loop-invariant-inference/test_results_20260524_074714/System/GlibC/changed_codes/list/multi_merge/`
**Result:** `QCP_VERIFIED: NO`

**What was attempted:**

1. **Step 1 (Symbolic Execution & State Collection):** The loop was unrolled (switch-based pattern) at depths 1, 2, and 3. Symexec crashed with a fatal error (`variable not instantiated`) at the post-loop `merge(y,z)` call in all cases, before producing any recording points. Only `RP_pre_loop` was recovered by parsing the log.

2. **Step 2 (Invariant Inference):** The loop splices one node from `y` and one node from `z` between elements of list `x`, then advances `t = u, u = u->next`. The intended invariant structure is:
   - A segment from `x` to the current tail `t` (dllseg_shape)
   - The remaining list from `u = t->next` (dlistrep_shape)
   - The unprocessed `y` and `z` lists

3. **Step 3 (Verification):** Multiple invariant formulations were tried:

   | Attempt | Invariant | Error |
   |---------|-----------|-------|
   | 1 | `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u) * dlistrep_shape(u, t) * ...` | Assign Exec fail: cannot find `data_at(t->next)` — `t` is arg[3] of dllseg, not arg[0] |
   | 2 | `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t) * dlistrep_shape(u, t) * ...` | Cannot find program variable `u` |
   | 3 | Same + `dlistrep_shape(u, t)` | Assign Exec fail at `t->next = y` |
   | 4 | `dllseg_shape(x, 0, u->prev, u) * dlistrep_shape(u->next, u) * ...` | Cannot find program variable `t` |
   | 5 | Same + `t == u->prev` in PROP | Assign Exec fail at `t->next = y` |
   | 6 | `dlistrep_shape(t, p) * dlistrep_shape(u, t) * ...` | Assign Exec fail — overlap between the two dlistrep shapes |
   | 7 | `dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) * ... && t == u->prev` | Timed out at invariant entry check |

**Root cause:** QCP's assignment execution requires explicit `data_at` for heap fields being written (e.g., `t->next`). These explicit `data_at` entries can only be created through C field expressions in predicate arguments. However, using field expressions creates `dup_data_at` conflicts with folded shape predicates that encode the same fields internally. The strategy system does not unfold predicates (like dllseg_shape) on demand for assignment pre-condition checking. For this complex three-list interleaving merge, no shape-predicate arrangement was found that simultaneously provides explicit field-level permissions without duplication.
